Governance file — GitHub + project knowledge. NOT precached; never
triggers a CACHE bump. Single authoritative list of everything open.
Convention: every session close updates this file. Closed items move to
the CLOSED LOG (never deleted). One line of truth per item — detail
lives in the owning document, not here.
Entry shape (normalised 29 Jul 2026, OI-59) — three lines, in this order:
  line 1   OI-n Title (the technical name — codes and cross-references)
  line 2   WHAT: one plain-English line, no codes, readable by ANYONE
  line 3   OWNER · GATE/DEADLINE · SOURCE
  line 4   STATUS — followed by any narrative
  line 5   CONFIRMED <date> (source) · NEXT-DUE: <trigger/cadence/review>
           (added 30 Jul 2026, AoT S5, CC-8 fix — every entry now carries the
           date its status was last confirmed and when it next needs checking;
           STANDING items carry a cadence, WAITING-DATE items their trigger)

THE «WHAT:» LINE IS NOT DECORATION (adopted 29 Jul 2026). The titles in this
register are written in project shorthand — RA-, EX-, TA-, R-numbers, § refs —
which is efficient for the people who wrote them and opaque to everyone else,
including the second crew member on whom RA-8 solo capability depends. Every
entry therefore carries a plain-English line naming the actual job. It is what
the dashboard displays as the heading; the technical title sits under it.
STATUS ALWAYS STANDS ON ITS OWN LINE and never abuts the finding text it
annotates. An annotation prepended to an older sentence inverts that
sentence's meaning — see OI-36, repaired 29 Jul 2026.

OI-113 Chassis baseline — rear spring hangers, photographed workshop inspection
      (+ bellhousing drain-hole look for the rear main seal)
      WHAT: Have a workshop check the chassis above the rear springs for
            cracks and photograph it, so later damage can be told from old
      NEIL (workshop) · pre-departure · R25 Chassis section; LC_Service_history_FINAL.xlsx Services (Jan 2026 broken rear leaf; five gearbox-off jobs 2022–2026)
      OPEN — opened 28 Sep 2026 (weekly scheduled research note, owner-approved).
      Cracking of the chassis rail at and above the rear spring hangers is the
      most serious structural issue reported on the 70 Series line, driven by
      overload plus corrugations. This truck is over GVM at full trim (OI-103,
      Facts §1) and broke a rear leaf in Jan 2026. A crack found in the field can
      only be judged against a record of how the rail looked before, and a
      baseline also exposes any old repair hidden under paint. SCOPE: clean the
      rail around the front and rear spring hangers on both sides; inspect
      flanges, side faces, holes, weld toes and hanger fixings; photograph both
      sides; file the photos with the service records. WHILE UNDERNEATH: note
      whether the bellhousing drain hole is dry — the rear main seal has no
      replacement record in the ledger although the gearbox came off in Apr
      2022, Apr 2023, Feb 2024, Feb 2025 and Feb 2026; owner reports no visible
      leak (28 Sep 2026). Doctrine: R25 Chassis; R20 §1.15. CLOSES ON: a Services
      (or Advisories) row recording the inspection and its finding, and the
      photos on file (E1). Any crack found becomes its own item.
      12 Oct 2026 (owner ruling, expert-sources pass): ALSO ASK THE WORKSHOP
      whether the 1HZ bellhousing drain hole takes a wading plug (fit one or
      carry one if so), and whether the timing-belt case can take in water on
      a deep crossing — interference engine (R28 §4; St Pierre White 2014,
      p. 189; Sheppard 2008, §5.3-34). Scope widened; status unchanged.
      CONFIRMED 28 Sep 2026 (owner approval) · NEXT-DUE: pre-departure (any workshop visit with the vehicle on a hoist)

OI-125 1HZ fitted thermostat rating, injection-pump ACSD, engine oil capacity and pre-heating system — confirm against Toyota RM617E
      WHAT: At the next workshop visit, read the temperature stamped on the fitted thermostat
            and ask whether the injection pump has a cold-start advance device;
            and settle how much engine oil this engine takes with a filter change;
            and note which glow plugs and pre-heating system it has
      NEIL (workshop) · same visit as OI-122, before the next trip (date not set) · LC76_Facts.txt §5 'Toyota 1HZ standards'; LC76_Engine_Training.html §4.3; LC76_Cooling_System_Guide.html thermostat card; Toyota RM617E CO-12 and EM-12
      OPEN — opened 20 Oct 2026 (eighty-ninth session), from checking R4 against
      Toyota's engine manual RM617E (owner-supplied; written for the
      HZJ105/HDJ100). (1) THERMOSTAT: the library says 82 °C on eight pages (R4,
      the Cooling System Guide, the Bush Mechanics Guide, the Pre-Trip Protocol,
      Spares & Tools, R8) and R8 tells the reader not to fit a lower-temperature
      unit; Toyota's manual gives 74–78 °C opening and at least 10 mm lift at 90
      °C, and says the opening temperature is stamped on the thermostat. The
      HZJ76 may use a different unit, so nothing was changed beyond a 'to
      confirm' note on R4 and the Cooling System Guide. (2) INJECTION TIMING:
      Toyota's 1HZ plunger stroke is 0.65–0.71 mm with ACSD and 0.85–0.91 mm
      without (EM-12); which applies depends on whether this pump has ACSD. TO
      DO: read the thermostat's stamped temperature (or its part number) and ask
      the workshop whether the pump has ACSD; then make the eight thermostat
      pages agree with the fitted part and record both in Facts §5. CLOSES ON:
      both facts recorded with their source (E1 photo or E3 workshop note) and
      the thermostat pages edited.
      16 Nov 2026 (S44): no trip in December 2026 (owner: 'we're going nowhere in december') — the
      target moves from 'before the December 2026 trip' to the next trip, date not set. Nothing else changed.
      CONFIRMED 20 Oct 2026 (RM617E check) · NEXT-DUE: before the next trip (date not set)
      ANNOTATED 20 Oct 2026 (eighty-ninth session, line-by-line RM617E check): (3) OIL
      CAPACITY — R4 says 11.3 L with filter (from the owner's manual, with the oil
      cooler) and the Troubleshooting Guide 8.5 L; RM617E LU-3 gives 9.3 L with filter,
      8.0 L without and 10.4 L dry for the HZJ105 (the '8.5' matches its US-quart
      figure without filter). The HZJ76 owner's manual, or the litres on the next
      service invoice, settles it; nothing changed meanwhile. (4) The
      Troubleshooting Guide's thermostat card already reads 74–78 °C, so the library
      disagrees with itself as well as with R4. CLOSES ON (extended): the oil
      capacity also recorded with its source and R4 and the Troubleshooting Guide
      made to agree.
      ANNOTATED 20 Oct 2026 (owner: 'make sure you use 1hz specs'): (5) PRE-HEATING —
      RM617E's 'super glow' section (ST-1 to ST-3) is not labelled by engine; it serves
      the glow-plug engines (1HZ, 1HD-T) as fitted to the 105 Series. Its figures —
      plug ≈0.75 Ω at 20 °C, never over 11 V direct, 13 Nm, glow light 0.5–4.5 s,
      afterglow to ~120 s — are now labelled as 105 Series figures in R4, the
      Troubleshooting Guide, Spares & Tools and Facts §5. TO DO: the part number on a
      glow plug (or the workshop) confirms this vehicle's plug type and system. CLOSES
      ON (extended): the pre-heating system and plug type recorded with their source.
      ANNOTATED 21 Oct 2026 (ninetieth session, owner 'yes' to two-manual labelling): (6) A SECOND
      TOYOTA 1HZ MANUAL — the owner supplied Toyota's 1PZ/1HZ/1HD-T engine repair manual
      (first printed Feb 1990, 34th printing Aug 2001; HZJ70/73/75/80 Series; text layer).
      It AGREES with RM617E on compression, valve clearance, idle and maximum speed, the
      head (26 bolts, 69 Nm + 90° + 90°), the gear-and-belt drive, the rotor oil pump, the
      feed pump inside the injection pump, glow-plug and nozzle-to-head torques and the
      thermostat (74–78 °C). It DIFFERS on: nozzle opening pressure (1HZ: 142–152 bar new,
      132–152 bar reused, A-9 — RM617E 147–157 / 142–152); injection timing (1HZ plunger
      stroke 1.03–1.09 mm, A-3 — RM617E 0.65–0.71 with ACSD / 0.85–0.91 without); crank
      pulley 490 Nm (RM617E 430); sump drain plug 25 Nm (34.3); radiator cap 74–103 kPa,
      limit 59 (93–123, min 78); regulator 14.0–15.0 V at 25 °C (13.2–14.8 V); oil capacity
      HZJ70/73/75 9.5 L with filter, 8.2 L without (HZJ105 9.3 / 8.0); and pre-heating —
      super glow only for Europe and Australia, a fixed-delay system elsewhere (HZJ75 glow
      light about 6 s). Both figures are now shown, labelled by manual, in R4, the
      Troubleshooting Guide, the Fuel System Guide, the Cooling System Guide (whose
      88–108 kPa cap range matched neither manual) and Facts §5. CORRECTION to the
      twentieth-October record: the old library figures 135–155 kg/cm², 1.03–1.09 mm,
      ~75–105 kPa and 25 Nm were the 70 Series manual's 1HZ figures, not errors — the
      20 Oct notes calling the nozzle range a splice and the stroke 'no 1HZ entry' were
      wrong. The RM0584E online manual (toyotamanuals.gitlab.io) could not be reached from
      the session (empty page; network refused, 21 Oct 2026); the owner has no PDF of it.
      TO DO (extended): ask the workshop for the injector opening pressure and the pump

OI-130 Odometer error since the 265/75 tyres — measure it against GPS (speedometer not recalibrated)
      WHAT: Drive a long steady run with a GPS trip counter running, compare it with the
            odometer, and record how far out the odometer reads on the new, taller tyres
      NEIL · before the next trip (date not set) (suggested) · LC76_Facts.txt §6 ('Speedometer' line); LC76_Fuel_Log_Analyser.html (tyre-change switch, TYRE_CHANGE factor)
      OPEN — opened 1 Nov 2026 (ninety-ninth session, owner ruling 'open an item for
      the gps check', E4). The BFG KO3 265/75R16 tyres fitted 17 Sep 2026 @ 314,624 km
      replaced 265/70R16s and the speedometer was not recalibrated (owner, 1 Nov 2026).
      Nominal diameters 777.4 mm and 803.9 mm give +3.4%, but that is arithmetic from
      the size code, not a measurement: the speedometer's own calibration was never
      recorded. WHY IT MATTERS: if the odometer under-reads, every km-since service
      figure in Facts runs short — services come due later in real km than the page
      says (about 33 km so far; roughly 2,000 km over a 60,000 km expedition) — and the
      fuel log reads economy low. TO DO: (1) reset a GPS trip counter (phone app or a
      Garmin unit) and note the odometer; (2) drive at least 50 km of steady, mostly
      straight tar; (3) note both readings; (4) repeat on another run; (5) error % =
      (GPS km - odometer km) / odometer km x 100, the two runs averaged. A GPS track
      slightly under-counts on bends and climbs, which is why a long, straight run is
      used. CLOSES ON: both runs' readings recorded in Facts §6 (E2), the analyser's
      tyre factor set to the measured figure, and the owner's ruling (E4) on what the
      error changes — recalibrate the speedometer, or adjust the service intervals in
      Facts and the Service Checklist by it.
      16 Nov 2026 (S44): no trip in December 2026 (owner: 'we're going nowhere in december') — the
      target moves from 'before the December 2026 trip' to the next trip, date not set. Nothing else changed.
      CONFIRMED 1 Nov 2026 (owner ruling) · NEXT-DUE: before the next trip (date not set)

OI-83 House-battery capacity-fade sensing gap — no capacity-test cadence

            comes up short

      OPEN — G5 finding. The BMV-712 Smart reads SOC and voltage but nothing measures
      actual CAPACITY, so LiFePO4 fade is invisible until a multi-day camp runs
      short earlier than the R7 model predicts. RA-2 baselines the engine, not the
      battery. Set a periodic full-cycle capacity check (annually / at major
      services), record the Ah delivered against the 210 Ah nameplate, and trend
      it in R7.

OI-84 Alternator load headroom at idle — does the crank battery gain at idle?
      WHAT: Confirm the crank battery actually charges at idle with the DC-DC
            running, not only at road speed
      NEIL (measure) · next service / pre-departure · G6 systemic sweep (AoT S7) · R32 / R26
      OPEN — G6 limit-proximity finding. The Orion DC-DC is capped at ~40A to
      protect the 1HZ alternator, but at IDLE the alternator's output falls well
      below its rating; 40A to the house bank plus vehicle loads could exceed idle
      output, so the CRANK battery discharges while the driver believes it is
      charging. Measure crank-battery current at idle with the DC-DC active (clamp
      meter); if negative, add an idle-charging note/limit to R32. Ties to the
      crank-survival chain (OI-46).
      ANNOTATED 24 Oct 2026 (ninety-fourth session): Facts §8, R5 and R26 give the
      Orion output cap as ~30 A; this entry says ~40 A. The figure is read off the
      unit under OI-127 (1).
      4 Nov 2026 (S37): not checked yet (owner).
      ANNOTATED 18 Nov 2026 (S68, Fix 3): the alternator is 80 A (owner, E4; Facts §8 —
      Toyota offered 55, 80 or 100 A); the LiFePO4 calculator now defaults to the ~30 A cap

OI-86 LiFePO4 low-temp charge cutoff — sub-zero morning planning
      WHAT: Plan for the house battery refusing to charge on frosty mornings
      NEIL+CLAUDE · document pre-Karoo · G7 systemic sweep (AoT S7) · R7 / R5
      OPEN — G7 finding. The house LiFePO4 BMS blocks charging below ~0–5 °C. On
      sub-zero mornings (Sutherland and highland camps on the Karoo loop, and
      later high-altitude nights) solar and DC-DC charging will NOT resume until
      the pack self-warms, so a cold dawn starts with no charge input. Document
      the expectation and the mitigation (battery insulation/box, or drive to warm
      the pack) in R7/R5. Bites on the imminent Karoo winter trip, not just the
      expedition.
      CONFIRMED 30 Jul 2026 (AoT S7) · NEXT-DUE: pre-Karoo (document + mitigate)

OI-93 Home-side legal & insurance continuity — house cover, wills, PoA holder
      (SIGNIFICANT, legality)
      WHAT: Make sure the legal and insurance situation at home survives a
            long absence
      NEIL+MARY-ANN · pre-departure · G11 systemic sweep (AoT S8) · R38 §1 / R39 §6 gap
      OPEN — SIGNIFICANT (legality). Genuinely untracked. R38 §1 names the
      anchor system; R39 §6 covers money strategy on the road; nothing covers
      the home-side legal continuity. Three specific classes: (1) HOUSE-OWNER
      INSURANCE — SA policies commonly cap unoccupied periods at 30 / 60 / 90
      days; a 24-month absence without a declared arrangement voids fire /
      burglary cover. (2) WILLS — currency of both crew's wills, executor
      availability, both-crew-simultaneous-absence contingency. (3) POWER OF
      ATTORNEY holder currency — the person holding your authority may fall ill,
      die, or become uncontactable; named succession. This is the LEGAL mirror
      of the RC1 / name discipline done 28 Jul: aggregation risk was about what
      LEAVES; this is about what STAYS.
      19 Nov 2026 (S75, OI-135): WHAT made length-neutral (owner: the trip's
      length is open); it read 'survives a 24-month absence'.
      CONFIRMED 30 Jul 2026 (AoT S8) · NEXT-DUE: pre-departure

OI-90 Long-duration crew wellbeing — monthly check-in cadence (chronic fatigue,
      decision quality, accumulated friction)
      WHAT: Set up a monthly review of how we are travelling with each other —
            not just the day-to-day rest routine
      NEIL+MARY-ANN · pre-departure build · G9 systemic sweep (AoT S8) · R38 §5 gap
      OPEN — R38 §5 covers ACUTE rest & pace (5–7 day rest-day cadence, no night
      driving, share-the-load, the discretionary-risk veto). LONG-DURATION crew
      wellbeing is not covered anywhere — chronic fatigue that erodes decisions
      over months, accumulated friction, a check on whether the standing
      routines are still working after 6 / 12 / 18 months. Long-duration
      two-person expedition literature (Sheppard, Scott, polar research) puts
      interpersonal drift and decision degradation ABOVE mechanical failure as
      the top mission-endangering factor. Define a monthly check-in (30 min,
      minuted like the suspend/abort log per §2), define the signals that
      warrant an off-schedule check (either crew may call one), and hook the
      output into R23 post-trip review.
      CONFIRMED 30 Jul 2026 (AoT S8) · NEXT-DUE: build before departure

OI-92 In-trip leg-reroute framework — distinct from suspend/abort (G10)
      WHAT: Decide in advance how we will change our onward route mid-trip
            (short of aborting) if a country turns bad
      NEIL+CLAUDE · pre-departure build · G10 systemic sweep (AoT S8) · R38 §2/§6 gap
      OPEN — R38 §2 defines SUSPEND (pause) and ABORT (end the leg or trip). §6
      A8 lists route-security as an assumption reviewed each rest day (travel
      advisories, EX-10 refresh, local intelligence). What is NOT framed is the
      RE-ROUTE decision — abandoning a planned onward corridor for an
      alternate one without ending the trip. Cabo Delgado 2020–24 is the case
      study: a leg that could be re-routed rather than aborted. Consolidate into
      a small addition to R38 §2 or §6: named forward-scan sources, cadence
      (weekly? bi-weekly? for legs 2–3 ahead), and thresholds distinguishing
      re-route from suspend from abort. Feeds EX-9.
      CONFIRMED 30 Jul 2026 (AoT S8) · NEXT-DUE: pre-departure (EX-9 input)

OI-95 R38 §2 abort domain gap — FAMILY and HOME-SIDE triggers
      WHAT: Add the two missing columns to R38 §2's abort-and-suspend table

      OPEN — R38 §2 is a real, built framework: suspend vs abort, asymmetric

      home loss). Same style and depth as the existing four: suspend threshold,

▌C. SMALL FIXES & DEFECTS


OI-46 Confirm how the ARB jump pack is recharged in the field
      WHAT: Confirm how the jump pack gets recharged out in the field
      NEIL (verify) · before departure · Opened 24 Jul 2026, from the RA-4 ruling
      OPEN — With no spare alternator carried (RA-4 ruled 24 Jul 2026), the
      ARB lithium jump pack is promoted from convenience to CRITICAL PATH.
      R32 is explicit that the alternator is the crank battery's only
      charge source: solar and shore power both serve the house bank, the
      Orion is one-way crank->house, and starting off the LiFePO4 is
      prohibited. So after an alternator failure the jump pack is the only
      way the engine starts, for as long as it takes to reach a
      replacement.
      THE QUESTION: can the pack itself be recharged in the field from the
      house bank — 12 V socket or mains via the MultiPlus — and how long
      does it take? If YES, the survival chain is solar -> house bank ->
      jump pack -> crank, and that chain must be written into R32 as
      doctrine, because at present nobody has stated it anywhere. If NO,
      the ruling leaves a hole that has to be filled another way (a small
      standalone crank maintainer, a second pack, or reinstating the spare
      alternator decision).
      Cheap to answer: read the pack's manual and test it once. Record the
      method and the recharge time in R32.
      4 Nov 2026 (S37): MORE CRITICAL — the owner rules the ARB starter pack is THE way to start
      on a flat crank battery (OI-115 closed; the Orion reverse charge is not used). The
      field-recharge question here is now the only open link in that chain.
      CONFIRMED 4 Nov 2026 (owner ruling, OI-115) · NEXT-DUE: pre-departure (owner test)

OI-103 GVM overload — measured +280 kg over at full expedition trim (DECISION)
      WHAT: The fully-loaded vehicle weighs 280 kg more than its legal maximum —
            decide how to make it legal
      NEIL · pre-departure (decision), informs any rear-suspension work · weighbridge 15 Sep 2026 (E1), Facts §1
      OPEN — DECISION, SIGNIFICANT (legality + insurance + feasibility). The
      15 Sep weighbridge measured 3,280 kg gross in full expedition trim vs the
      3,000 kg GVM — over by 280 kg (9.3%) whenever fully provisioned. Legal on
      each axle alone (rear 2,120/2,180, front 1,160/1,480) but not on GVM. Two
      honest routes to legal: (a) a payload audit to shed ~280 kg (R11 exercise
      — moving dense mass forward relieves the rear axle but does NOT cut the
      total, so weight must actually come off); (b) a certified/engineered GVM
      re-rating (a documented re-rating, not a spring swap). COUPLED
      CONSIDERATION (owner-raised): the rear sags visibly under load, no handling
      change. A rear leaf / helper / heavier OME pack restores stance and
      durability but does NOT raise the legal ceiling — it treats the symptom,
      not the finding; if the rear is touched, match the pack to the measured
      load and treat compliance as this decision, not a spring purchase. Bears on
      OI-104 propagation and the Cross Country insurance rewrite (over-GVM =
      repudiation ground, R29). Splitting the sag/spring into its own item is
      available on request.
      STRUCTURAL DIMENSION (added 28 Sep 2026): the overload is not only a
      legal and insurance question. Sustained load at or over GVM plus
      corrugations is the reported driver of chassis cracking above the rear
      spring hangers on the 70 Series line; the OME lift does not change that.
      Baseline inspection: OI-113. Doctrine: R25 Chassis section. Any engineered
      GVM re-rating (route b) is a judgement about this same chassis.
      4 Nov 2026 (S37): the second spare carcass on the roof (OI-19, decided) was not in the
      10 Aug 2026 weighing; it adds one tyre's weight, high up, to the expedition trim.
      CONFIRMED 4 Nov 2026 (OI-19 decided) · NEXT-DUE: pre-departure decision

CONTROLLED DUPLICATION REGISTER (adopted 23 Jul 2026, seeded 24 Jul)

A short, deliberate list of facts permitted to exist in more than one
document. Each has ONE named OWNER and a mandatory PROPAGATION LIST.
Any change to a registered fact triggers a sweep of that entire list in
the SAME session — not a follow-up item, not a note for next time. This
converts a latent seam into a lookup, which is the same move as the
R30 owner-flag column (approved in principle, deferred past the Karoo
loop). The content audit's central finding was that this library's
weakness is its seams; this is the control aimed directly at them.

  SSOT — ABSORBED BY THE FACT STORE (18 Sep 2026, OI-107). Under the ratified
  single-source-of-truth doctrine the CDR is being COMPLETED and absorbed by the
  fact store in LC76_Facts.txt: each registered fact becomes a store entry with an
  owner and (if derived) a formula, and the release+gate pipeline enforces the
  propagation the CDR used to prompt by hand. The CDR was a REACTIVE control —
  rows added only after a fact had already drifted (the odometer was never even a
  row); the store makes it complete and automatic. MIGRATED SO FAR: the odometer

            Closes on a stronger protector bought (Facts §11B and R9 updated) or the margin
            accepted by the owner in writing.

OI-99 Explanatory-depth ("gems") sweep — surface compressed assertions that
       state a rule without the mechanism that makes it usable
       WHAT: Sweep the library for statements that assert a behaviour or rule
             but omit the WHY that lets a reader act on it correctly — starting
             with the electrical documents, then library-wide
       NEIL+CLAUDE · post-Karoo · owner request 5 Aug 2026 (this session, from the cold-charge and switch-position corrections) · R5/R7/R26 pilot cases
       WAITING-DATE — Held until after the Karoo loop. ORIGIN: two corrections
       this session exposed a pattern. The cold-charge line ("avoid charging
       until above 0°C") read as a manual instruction when the BMS does it
       automatically; the §13 switch positions were inferred, not confirmed.
       Both are the same class: a fact recorded without the mechanism that makes
       it operable, which reads as authority and can mislead. THE RISK is
       highest for Mary-Ann, on whom RA-8 solo capability depends — a compressed
       line she cannot unpack is a latent gap. SCOPE: a measured sweep (candidates
       listed, auditable like every other finding, not vibes) for assertions of
       the form "do X / don't do Y / X happens" that omit the mechanism; for each,
       decide whether a short "why this works" note is warranted and, per the
       ownership-first convention, WHERE it belongs (the owning R-doc — the terse
       fact stays as the reference, the mechanism note makes it teachable, the
       two-tier pattern). RUN ELECTRICAL-FIRST as the pilot (R5/R7/R26/R32 +
       the calculators) to prove the pattern on one domain before committing to
       the whole corpus; charter the library-wide pass off the pilot's result.
       NOTE the meta-lesson recorded with the switch fix: build the sweep on
       facts CONFIRMED against the vehicle, not inferred from product-general
       behaviour. Feeds no gate but should precede EX-11 (the printed field pack)
       so the laminated extracts carry the explained versions.

      ORIGINAL ENTRY (verbatim, as it stood open):
OI-60 Print standard has never taken effect — library-wide
      WHAT: Fix printing across the whole library; pages print at screen
            size
      CLAUDE (build) · programme, size ruled by owner · diagnostic 29 Jul 2026
      OPEN.
      Every font size in the library is declared in rem, which resolves
      against the ROOT element. Every @media print block sets body. Body is
      not root, so for rem-sized text the print rule does nothing at all and
      never has. Printed pages have been coming out at screen size.
      DIAGNOSTIC, 29 Jul 2026, depth-aware parse of all 71 HTML files at
      pinned HEAD: ZERO files set the root size in print; 1,547 rem
      font-size declarations sit in base CSS across the corpus. Severity
      varies by how far each file compensates with per-element overrides —
      52 barely or not at all, 9 partial, 9 meaningful, 1 unaffected (the
      affidavit, which sizes nothing). The universal defect is all 71.
      REMEDY, PROTOTYPED IN LC76_How_To_Use_This_Library.html THE SAME DAY:
      one root declaration inside @media print, plus a shared block of rem
      heading overrides that COMPRESS the hierarchy (on screen a heading
      earns its size; on paper it need only be distinguishable). Because
      the overrides are rem and not pt, the root value is a single knob that
      rescales the document with relationships intact. No anchor hazard —
      nothing is replaced, only added.
      OWNER GATE: body size ruled off a real printout before propagation.
      12pt trialled and rejected as too large; 8pt adopted on the guide
      29 Jul 2026 (knob = 11.6px). Micro-labels fall to ~5pt at that setting
      and the owner has accepted them.
      PROPAGATION: Design Guidelines §16 must be corrected FIRST — it
      currently specifies the body-pt form that cannot work — then the
      template, then the corpus. The 9 partially-compensated files need
      their existing overrides reviewed as well as the root added, and are
      a separate, more careful pass.
      CONFIRMED 30 Jul 2026 (AoT S5) · NEXT-DUE: remediation programme — DG §16 first
      NOTE 4 Aug 2026 (Karoo update session): two files now carry WORKING print
      blocks AHEAD of the DG §16 correction and the formal remedy — the guide
      (How_To_Use, the 29 Jul prototype) and now LC76_Karoo_Winter_Loop_2026.html.
      KAROO USED THE R10 PER-ELEMENT PATTERN, NOT THE rem-KNOB REMEDY, DELIBERATELY:
      owner asked for "same as R10", and Karoo's base CSS is sized in PX, not rem,
      so the single-root-knob remedy cannot rescale it until the base CSS is first
      converted px→rem. Rendered proof (headless Chromium, print media) 4 Aug:
      the Karoo printout dropped 20 pages → 8, open whitespace closed, headings
      compressed to R10 density; body ~7pt, micro-labels ~5pt as accepted on the
      guide. THIS IS AN ACCEPTED INTERIM, NOT THE PROPAGATION PATTERN — a future
      OI-60 pass MUST NOT rip the Karoo/R10 px block out as "non-conforming", and
      MUST NOT copy it as the corpus template; it converts Karoo's base CSS to rem
      and folds it into the shared override block like every other file. Karoo
      therefore joins the "9 meaningful per-element-compensated" class (with R10),
      not the rem-knob class. ADDED FINDING, prerequisite the diagnostic did not
      surface: the remedy assumes rem sizing corpus-wide ("1,547 rem declarations"),
      but at least some library files are PX-sized (Karoo confirmed), so the
      eventual programme carries a PER-FILE px→rem base-CSS conversion step wherever
      a file is px-sized — a bigger job than a print-block swap, sequenced into the
      "9 meaningful / 52 barely" tranching. OI-60 STAYS OPEN.
      CONFIRMED 4 Aug 2026 (Karoo session) · NEXT-DUE: remediation programme — DG §16 first

OI-96 Print grayscale contrast — muted labels fade in black-and-white
      WHAT: Make the library print legibly on a black-and-white printer
      CLAUDE (build) · programme, after Karoo pattern proven · Karoo B&W print report 5 Aug 2026 (owner), rendered-grayscale diagnostic
      OPEN. A SEPARATE AXIS FROM OI-60 (which is print FONT-SIZE, rem-vs-root).
      This is print CONTRAST: the design's small mono labels are set in muted
      tokens — amber/ochre (--ochre #D4963A) and smoke — which collapse to light
      grey when a printer converts colour to grayscale. Measured on the Karoo
      file: the ochre eyebrow labels fall to grey L≈157 on an L≈237 ground (Δ80,
      the faint text the owner reported); the fuel-label is rgba(...,0.6) on a
      dark fill and VANISHES entirely if "print background graphics" is off.
      Two failure modes: (1) muted colours too light in grayscale; (2) light-on-
      dark text depending on backgrounds printing. FIX PATTERN PROVEN ON KAROO
      (owner ruling 5 Aug: option B): inside @media print only, drop every dark
      fill to white, force all text to solid black at full opacity, restore the
      dark banners' structure as black rules/borders, and nudge the two tiniest
      labels 5pt→6pt. Screen/mobile/colour print untouched. Rendered proof

29 Jul 2026  OI-54 · LIBRARY-WIDE TOC WRAP DEFECT — SINGLE-ROW SCROLLER
      PROMOTED TO THE BASE RULE; DESIGN GUIDELINES v1.8
      OPENED AND CLOSED SAME DAY, over four staged passes. RAISED BY THE
      OWNER against the Karoo itinerary: the TOC wrapped to three rows on
      a laptop while behaving correctly on mobile. ROOT CAUSE: the
      single-row horizontal scroller was scoped to a max-width media
      query, so desktop fell through to a base rule declaring
      flex-wrap:wrap. Because the TOC sits inside the mandatory sticky
      unit, every wrapped row permanently consumed viewport on every
      screen of the document.
      SCALE: 29 of 61 files with a TOC declared flex-wrap:wrap; 20 of
      those overflowed a 1440px viewport. Only FOUR files (R4, R8, R19,
      Seal Gasket Atlas) carried the scroller in the base rule — those
      four supplied the pattern, so the remedy is ADOPTION, NOT
      INVENTION. R4 Engine Training, with 75 TOC buttons and a ~15,400px
      row, was the strongest evidence: it works precisely because its
      base rule is correct.
      OWNER RULINGS (29 Jul): scope = normalise the whole library, not
      only the visibly broken files, because a wrapping TOC is LATENT
      until someone adds a section — which is exactly how the Karoo file
      broke, three weeks after being fine. Scrollbar stays HIDDEN at all
      widths, matching the four proven files; a pointer:fine slim
      scrollbar was built, offered and REJECTED, then reverted out of the
      Karoo file. The 80 KB own-session rule is WAIVED for anchored
      CSS-only edits, extending the existing masthead-migration exemption
      — nine affected files exceed 80 KB and the edit is 2–4 anchored
      strings plus a full validation walk, identical in shape.
      EXECUTED IN FOUR PASSES, 39 files: S1 Profile TEMPLATE + Design
      Guidelines (template first, so nothing inherits the defect; the
      standard written into DG S6 and the document bumped v1.7 → v1.8);
      S2 ten country/terrain profiles (masthead bumps + changelog entries
      in BOTH formats, per the OI-39 divergence); S3 six remaining
      overflowing files; S4 ten latent files. CLOSING CORPUS ASSERT
      PASSES: no file wraps its TOC at desktop width, and no file lacks a
      scroll mechanism. 33 files now carry the standard explicitly; 28
      are correct by CSS default and were deliberately LEFT UNTOUCHED —
      editing working files for cosmetic uniformity is churn.
      FOUR PROBE/GUARD ERRORS, ALL CAUGHT BEFORE DAMAGE, disclosed
      because the pattern is the lesson: (1) the first TOC survey matched
      only <nav class="toc"> and reported ONE affected file when most of
      the library uses .toc-bar; (2) the idempotence guard fired on
      Angola for overscroll-behavior-x:contain, which also sits on
      .table-scroll under the standing inner-scroller pattern; (3) the
      changelog anchor assumed a wrapper style that varies between
      profiles; (4) MOST SERIOUS — the category classifier took the FIRST
      .toc-bar match in the flattened CSS, but in many files that match
      is inside a media query. Rebuilt with depth-aware parsing (a rule
      is BASE only when braces balance to zero before it), which
      DISSOLVED "Category C" entirely: all 13 files so classified were
      already correct, and would have been edited for nothing. S4 fell
      from 23 files to 10. STANDING LESSON REINFORCED: when a probe
      disagrees with the file, suspect the probe — and a CSS probe must
      respect nesting, not text order.
      FLAGGED, NOT FIXED (needs an owner ruling): FIVE files carry no
      masthead "Last updated" line — the Manifesto, the Manifesto
      Commentary, History of Overlanding and Expedition as a System (all
      Philosophy, which IS in the DG S10 closed vocabulary and should
      carry one) plus the Content Audit (governance, legitimately
      exempt). Same defect class as the Karoo masthead the owner ruled on
      28 Jul. SECOND GAP: non-profile R-docs carry no changelog section,
      so the standing rule that every masthead bump carries a matching
      changelog entry cannot be satisfied for them — it needs either an
      exemption or changelogs added.
      Content and CSS only — CACHE STAYS v24, no file added, removed or
      renamed. Next free OI-55.

27 Jul 2026  OI-49 · PROJECT-KNOWLEDGE CURRENCY — STALE GOVERNANCE FOSSILS
      REMOVED. OPENED AND CLOSED SAME DAY (owner removed the files on
      the spot). FOUND while verifying that the updated Project
      Instructions had reached the instructions field: the project
      KNOWLEDGE file set still held three governance copies, all badly
      stale — LC76_Facts.txt 20.6 KB dated 18 Jun 2026 (live: 28.4 KB,
      24 Jul), LC76_Library_Register.txt 18.6 KB dated 14 Jun (live:
      38.8 KB, 10 Jul), LC76_Project_Instructions.txt 14.3 KB dated
      23 Jun (live: 200.8 KB, 26 Jul — the fossil was SEVEN PER CENT of
      the real file). The June Facts copy predates the entire Victron
      LiFePO4 install, the GVM 3,000 kg correction, the licence-date
      corrections and the 65.3 Ah/day load supersession.
      WHY IT HAD NOT YET BITTEN, AND WHY THAT IS NOT REASSURANCE: the
      standing convention "never edit against a project-knowledge copy —
      always fetch live, SHA-pinned" had been holding the hazard back.
      That is a discipline suppressing a defect, not the absence of one.
      The specific exposure was model routing: the Project Instructions
      themselves send "Facts.txt queries" to the fastest tier for quick
      lookups, and a quick lookup against a six-week-old Facts returns a
      confident wrong answer about the electrical system with no fetch
      and no seam visible to the reader.
      RESOLUTION: all three removed from project knowledge by the owner,
      27 Jul 2026. GitHub is now the only copy of each, which forces a
      fetch rather than permitting a stale read. The Project Instructions
      remain in the instructions FIELD (a different mechanism — injected
      automatically each session, and verified current at the eighteenth
      session header). New standing convention recorded in the Project
      Instructions: project knowledge carries NO duplicate of a
      governance file that lives on GitHub; if one is ever added it is
      refreshed in the same session as the GitHub edit, or removed.
      GENERAL LESSON: this library's failure mode is the seam, and a
      stale duplicate is a seam that reports no error. A copy that
      DOES NOT EXIST forces the correct behaviour; a copy that exists and
      is wrong invites the incorrect one. Prefer absence to staleness.

      METHOD: blocks were extracted verbatim and re-assembled, with
      assertions that no item was filed twice, that the union of filed

      because it is the same lesson as F65 in a different costume: the
      defect was introduced by an edit that PASSED every validation gate

      highest-value element on an emergency card, because under stress
      people narrate instead of reporting and forget the coordinates;

      the failure mode is a careful person behaving carefully, and
      entitlement is recoverable after the fact while physiology is not.

      one-owner rule upheld. PROCESS DEFECT DISCLOSED: the first edit
      pass carried an emoji written as a lone surrogate pair, which threw
      on encode AFTER open(...,'w') had already truncated the file to
      zero bytes. No data was lost — the file was restored from the
      pinned SHA in one fetch, which is precisely why the pinning
      discipline exists — but the edit pattern was hardened the same
      session to ENCODE FIRST and write binary, so a bad encode can never
      again destroy the target before it fails. VALIDATION: depth walks
      0/0/0 across div/span/table/tr/td/th/thead/tbody/p/li/ul; 13 ids

22 Jul 2026  OI-38 CLOSURE CORRECTED — THE ENTRY BELOW WAS WRONG AND IS
      SUPERSEDED BY THIS ONE. It is retained rather than deleted because
      the error is the useful record. WHAT WENT WRONG: the contract was
      taken to be "sectionIds must mirror the TOC buttons", verified on
      that basis, and applied family-wide. That is only half the
      contract. The other half is the TOGGLE EXPRESSION that consumes the
      pairing, and it was NOT uniform: 001–006 and the template use
      i===idx, while 007–010 used (i-1)===idx, a convention under which
      an array that deliberately OMITS the leading Alerts button is
      CORRECT. CONSEQUENCE: 007 Mozambique, 008 Malawi and 009 Zimbabwe
      were correct before the "fix" and were BROKEN BY IT — prepending
      'alerts' to their arrays put every section one button out. Those
      three files were pushed to the repo in that state. 005 Kalahari
      (i===idx) was genuinely broken and the fix was right. 010 Kenya was
      genuinely broken, was never touched, and the original finding holds
      for it alone. ROOT CAUSE: a family-wide rule asserted from a
      sample, without reading the code that consumes the data in each
      file — the same class of error as F65 (a plausible generalisation
      that reads as authority), and the reason working convention (d)
      exists. REPAIR (22 Jul, same day): rather than reverting, the
      convention was NORMALISED — the toggle in 007, 008, 009 and 010
      changed from (i-1)===idx to i===idx, which makes the already-
      altered arrays correct AND fixes 010's pre-existing defect. All
      ELEVEN files (ten profiles + template) verified together on one
      rule: toggle i===idx, sectionIds identical to the TOC targets in
      order and length, div/span walks 0/0/0, ids unique, node --check
      clean. LESSON RECORDED IN THE PI: the contract is array + TOC +
      TOGGLE, and the toggle must be read per file, never assumed.
      SAME PASS: changelog discipline applied per owner ruling — every
      masthead bump now carries a changelog entry, all profiles carry a
      changelog, dates normalised to ISO. Entries added to 005, 007, 008,
      009, 010; 010 masthead bumped. Content-currency badges deliberately
      NOT touched (document-level provenance, standing convention).
      Structural divergence in changelog form → OI-39. Content-only —
      CACHE stays v24.

22 Jul 2026  [SUPERSEDED BY THE ENTRY ABOVE — retained as the record of
      the error] OI-38 CLOSED — profile TOC / IntersectionObserver index
      contract swept across the whole profile family and repaired. The
      highlighter maps sectionIds.indexOf(id) onto tocLinks[i] BY
      POSITION, so the array must mirror the TOC click targets exactly in
      order and length; a mismatch does not throw, is invisible to node
      --check and to a depth walk, and simply highlights the wrong
      section. AUDIT METHOD (worth repeating): compare the sectionIds
      array against the getElementById targets extracted from every
      element carrying class="toc-link" — not against the button LABELS,
      and not with a regex that assumes <a> elements or a fixed attribute
      order. Two earlier extractor versions gave false readings: 009
      Zimbabwe uses <span class="toc-link" onclick=...> rather than <a>
      and read as "0 buttons", and a class-first pattern missed every
      file where class is the last attribute. Both were regex artifacts,
      caught before being reported. RESULT ACROSS ELEVEN FILES: four
      genuinely broken, all on the same 'alerts' mismatch — 007
      Mozambique, 008 Malawi and 009 Zimbabwe each had an Alerts button
      absent from the array; 005 Kalahari had the inverse, 'alerts' in
      the array with no button. All four fixed, mastheads bumped 22 Jul,
      validated (contract 20/20 with pairwise order, div/span walks
      0/0/0, ids unique, node --check clean). 010 Kenya was already
      correct and was not touched — the defect was not universal. Six
      files had been repaired earlier the same day during the OI-36
      build (001, 002, 003, 004, 006, TEMPLATE), so all eleven now hold
      the contract. Content-only — CACHE stays v24. Next free OI-39.
